At its 12th meeting, on 6 October 2021, the Rules of Procedure Commission considered the draft Supplement to the Rules of Procedure as a working body. Since the Commission has not adopted the draft Supplement to the Rules of Procedure with the necessary two-thirds majority of the members present after its final consideration, the second consideration of the Draft Supplement to the Rules of Procedure at the meeting of the working body has been completed. ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u2	President, thank you for your word. Your Honor! The first-signed drafter of the Supplement to the Rules of Procedure, which we are considering today, argues that without the proposed amendment he cannot carry out his work effectively, this is the conduct of a parliamentary inquiry to investigate the alleged money laundering in the NKBM and the disputed financing of the SDS party. The solution to the successful investigation of SDS financing is seen in the revision of the Rules of Procedure, which would also allow one of the investigative commissions to meet with two working bodies at the same time. The main task of the National Assembly is the enforcement of the legislative branch of power and therefore the rules of procedure give priority to the working body which adopts legislation rather than to the investigative commissions. The purpose of parliamentary investigations is in particular to identify the political responsibilities of the holders of public functions and powers. It therefore considers this institution to be a means of control or opposition or a legislative body as a whole over other institutions. The fact is that in this mandate, according to the previous number of parliamentary investigations, almost a double number of these are being investigated, since at present seven different areas are being investigated in the National Assembly and most of them have been caused by the affairs of the ruling party and these governments, both the controversial financing of the SDS party and the acquisition of protective equipment and the illicit political interference of the police. It also maintains the fact that the President of the individual Commission must manage the restrictions in order to work in a successful, timely and efficient manner. Therefore, taking into account the legislative advantages of the work, attention must also be paid to the possibility of members being involved, the State Assembly's timetable, and bearing in mind, however, that the mandate of the Commission of Inquiry is limited in time. As has already been pointed out, in this mandate there is an increase in the number of investigative commissions that carry out their work on a regular and regular basis. Therefore, we are rightly asked why the first-signed amendment proposal is the most hampered in investigating the alleged illegal financing of the SDS party, while other commissions are doing their work on a regular basis. In the SAB, we certainly support the Institute of Parliamentary Investigation, but at the same time, we understand the proposed amendment mainly as an excuse and an excuse for the first-signed proposer, why, under his leadership, the commission investigating the prime minister in the NKB and the related alleged financing of the SDS party does not meet regularly in this mandate and why, as it turns out, it will receive its epilogue. We have opposed the proposed changes. Thank you for your attention.

Members of the Desus Group see the Rules of Procedure as a good and final act. This is also illustrated by the fact that it is not often the subject of change. It is precisely the subject of this proposal to complement the Rules of Procedure, which is the number of working bodies of the National Assembly, which can be held at the same time, has been the subject of several debates, but the necessary majority has not yet been reached. The rules of procedure now in force provide for the simultaneous meetings of two working bodies. The proposals and initiatives went in the same direction as either three working bodies, or two working bodies and one investigative commission, or two working bodies and the KNOVS, as well as some similar options. This proposal introduces the possibility of a simultaneous meeting between two working bodies and one investigative commission. The fact is that the number of working bodies within the mandate increases directly to the number of investigative commissions. Currently, we have 13 working bodies, 8 committees, 2 subcommittees in the National Assembly, seven investigative committees. Not only the number of working bodies, but also the number of meetings of individual working bodies from the term of office increases, especially on the basis of requests for urgent or extraordinary meetings with very political themes, which often have nothing to do with the actual problems of citizens. In the Group of Members, we are aware of the purpose of limiting the simultaneous meetings of working bodies. We are also aware of the problems, the problems that the current restriction brings. This is mainly a problem in finding a free date for regular meetings of working bodies. On Mondays, when work is scheduled in the parliamentary groups, on Tuesdays, when work is scheduled in the parliamentary groups, on Thursday morning, when government meetings are scheduled and therefore the representatives of the ministries are detained. Additional problems arise from requests for emergency meetings to be held within 15 days, far exceeding the number of regular meetings. In the Desus Group, we regret that the proposal has not received sufficient support on the working body, as this makes it impossible to discuss both the definition of all other Members and Members. Personally, as a member of the Rules of Procedure Commission, the proposed solution is to be met simultaneously by two working bodies and one investigative commission, and I would support it, together with my fellow Members, in this Chamber. In the Desus Group, we believe that the proposed change in the direction of the simultaneous course of two working bodies linked to the legislative process and another working body which is not bound to the legislative process is an investigative commission to account for the quality and responsibility of the National Assembly as such. Thank you.

We have therefore supported this proposal in our parliamentary group. Once again, we would like to point out that the Institute of Parliamentary Investigation is established by Article 93 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia and is a means of controlling either the opposition or the entire National Assembly over the functioning of the other branches of government. The Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly in organising the work of the National Assembly or its working bodies do not sufficiently take into account the specificities of the work and the needs of the investigative commissions. The supervisory functions they perform cannot be equated and, in our opinion, cannot be limited to the business provisions due to the work of the other working bodies of the National Assembly, which are not required to carry out the legislative procedure, nor to other decisions under the competence of the National Assembly which do not have the nature of a parliamentary inquiry. Therefore, in the Group of Non-Aligned Members and Members of Parliament, despite the fact that the proposal has not received sufficient support, we will try to re-convince at least the working group of the Rules of Procedure Commission, which is preparing a broader set of additions and amendments to re-classify this proposal, as they also pointed out the problem of restricting the reference of working bodies. Thank you.

We have several simultaneous meetings of working bodies where we are members under the current business arrangements. The proposed business complement would certainly further deepen such problems. Thirdly, the working bodies of the National Assembly have a very important role to play after business arrangements. We often make decisions that can no longer be changed at the plenary session of the National Assembly. We are assessing the social democrats, which also makes this very important when we decide whether to change the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly and allow for the simultaneous session and decision-making of more than two working bodies of the National Assembly. On the other hand, this is a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the law on the organisation and financing of cultivation and education within the framework of a shorter procedure. The draft law was tabled by a group of 30 Members and Members with first-signed Yuri Lepo. In order to further explain the draft law, I give the floor to the representative of the proposer, Yuri Lep. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u20	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Mr Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues all have a good day! In Article 1 of the novel, we propose to amend Article 135 of the Law on the organisation and financing of education and education, by proposing a change in the purpose of school funds payments, so that funds from them can also be allocated to the participation of children, students and students from socially less conducive environments. They would be intended for activities related to the implementation of the public policy but not entirely financed by public funds. This would ensure equal opportunities for all participants. Many Slovenian schools and kindergartens have established funds which are basically designed to raise funds for extra-standard education activities, but are often intended to help socially weaker children and students. Some cannot afford expensive winters and flights. The total indicative value is between 120 and 240 euros. Many schools also organize other trips and camps where teachers combine technical, cultural and sports days. These activities are also financed by ensuring that families that cannot afford these costs are paid from these school funds. We find that such disbursements from the funds are not systematically regulated. Some schools allow it that way, but others don't. The rules are also very different. Applicants, we therefore propose uniform rules and conditions for obtaining such assistance that would apply to all school funds. Assistance would only be provided for the participation of children, pupils and students from socially less prosperous families in activities related to the implementation of the public school programme, unless it was financed entirely by public funds. In the second paragraph of Article 135 of the ZOFVI, we propose that, irrespective of the regulation of the law governing income, the beneficiary of donations from the intended part of the income for each year may also be a kindergarten fund or a school fund. Funds for the operation of the Funds are obtained mainly from contributions from parents and donations from companies. The proposed amendment of the law will also allow for funding from the so-called earmarked part of the income that can be used in accordance with the Income Act to finance the generally beneficial purposes of NGOs. We are of the opinion that, in addition to NGOs, these funds can at least partly be obtained by schools or school funds, as they carry out the same mission as humanitarian organisations when funds are also allocated to help socially disadvantaged families. However, in the transitional provision of the novel, it is proposed that, pending a different arrangement of the Income Act, irrespective of the provisions of Article 25 of the Income-2 Act, income as income relating to education shall not also be paid from payment to preschool children, pupils, students or students from the school fund. Teachers, principals and school workers are almost uniform in their opinion that schools should also be able to collect and pay untaxed aid, as is the case with humanitarian organisations. Schools should certainly be trusted by the State to really distribute aid to people who need it to solve school content and teaching aids. Such aid cannot in any way be regarded as income. Thus, we propose that monetary assistance for the purposes described should not come to the income base of parents for the payment of income. This derogation from the Income-2 Act applies from 1. 1. 2022. Payments to children from socially disadvantaged families are now not exempt from income payments, since this is not a legal basis, but are considered gifts. However, gifts are taxed on income if their annual value exceeds €84. However, since most of the remittances from the school fund exceed the amount recorded, it is legal to provide that all payments from the school fund can be fixed as tax exemptions with the Income Act. This amendment removes this unfair regulation and gives the legal basis for paying to socially weaker children, pupils and students the exemption of income payments. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u21	Thank you very much. The draft law was considered by the Committee on Education, Science, Sport and Youth as a parent labour force. I give the floor to President Ivi Dimic for the presentation of the Committee's report. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u22	Dear Vice-President, Honourable Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! At its 22nd regular session, on 30 September 2021, the Committee on Education, Science, Sport and Youth considered a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the organisation and financing of education and education submitted to the National Assembly by a group of Members and Members with the first-signed Yuri Lepo. At its 114th regular meeting on 31 August 2021, the College of Presidents adopted a decision to consider the draft law following a reduced procedure. The Committee was provided with material published on the website of the National Assembly. No amendments were tabled within the time limit and proposals were submitted for amendments to the Committee by unrelated Members, LMŠ and SD to Articles 1 and 2. The representative of the applicant said in the introduction that the draft law changes or specifies the purpose of the school funds more clearly. In addition to financing over-standard activities that have a general impact on better quality education for all children and pupils, the purpose of school funds is complemented by the way that funds from school funds can, under certain conditions, also be allocated to the participation of children, pupils and students from socially less conducive environments in those activities related to the implementation of the public policy, but not entirely financed by public funding. The beneficiaries of the cost of participation in individual activities may be those children, students or pupils who are granted the right to a snack subsidy, as a maximum percentage of the cost of lunch, under the law governing the exercise of rights from public funds. The representative of the Ministry of Education, Science and Sport explained that the Government does not support the proposed law. The proposed arrangement, whereby funds from the school fund would also be allocated to the participation of children, pupils and students from socially less conducive environments in activities related to the implementation of the public policy but not fully financed by public funds, is not appropriate as it would jeopardise the primary purpose of the school fund. He said that the school fund, for its primary purpose, was not aimed at solving the economically weak, so that, with the additional purpose of using resources, as proposed by the amendment to Article 135(1) of the ZOFVI, the decision-making autonomy of the Management Board of the School Fund would be affected when deciding which activities, equipment and other means of raising the standard of teaching are meaningful and necessary in the local environment from the School Fund and are for the benefit of all children, students or students, irrespective of social status. The Committee was given an opinion by the Legislative and Legal Service, which examined the draft law in terms of its compliance with the Constitution, the legal system and the legislative technical aspects. Her representative said that in her opinion they had pointed out the non-systemic regulation of tax matter in other laws. Such regulation reduces the transparency and integrity of the rule of law, which can also be disputed in terms of the rule of law. The Committee also received the opinion of the Commission of the National Council for Culture, Science, Education and Sport, which supports the proposed amendments. Her representative postponed the opinion and said, inter alia, that the Commission supported the proposaler's intention to follow the child's benefits by means of the fund in ensuring equal opportunities in education and education, irrespective of the child's social circumstances in which he lives. The debate focused on equal opportunities for all children in the school system. A member of the committee has been exposed to support the Government's intention to regulate matter in the Income Act, and until then the proposed law should be adopted. On the basis of Article 131, paragraph 8, of the Rules of Procedure, the Committee has drawn up and adopted amendments to Articles 1 and 2. Pursuant to the first paragraph of Article 133 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the text of the amended draft law which forms an integral part of this report is prepared. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u23	Thank you very much. State Secretary at the Ministry of Education, Science and Sport, Damir Orehovec will present the opinion of the Government. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u24	Thank you very much, Chairman, for your word. Ladies and gentlemen, Members of Parliament! With regard to the proposal for a law tabled by a group of Members and Members with the first-signed Mr Yuri Lepo, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia gave a written opinion and presented it specifically to the competent education committee. The proposal amends Article 135 of the Law on the Organisation and Financing of Education and Education, which governs the School Fund, extending the purpose of the School Fund. The School Fund is now intended to finance activities that are not an integral part of the education programme or are not financed by public funds. It is therefore intended to purchase superstandard equipment, raise the standard of teaching and so on. A proposal for an amendment to the ZOFVI would therefore add a humanitarian purpose. Other things that are changing, the school fund is also becoming a beneficiary of income work and it is also proposed to exempt income payments from the school fund, which is temporary as an exception to the Income Act, i.e. to a different arrangement in that law. ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u42	All in attendance, all in attendance! President, thank you for your word. I would briefly summarize the amendments to the Law on Protection against Ionising Radiation and Nuclear Safety, adopted in 2018 and updated for the first time in 2019. The main reason for this time’s additions is to comply with the European Commission’s Directive on fundamental safety standards for radiation protection. This will also address the violations we have had with the transposition of this Directive into our legal order. In addition, the provisions of the applicable law on regulated professions had to be properly prepared. A provision to verify the effectiveness of measures to reduce radon concentrations is also complemented. ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u44	Thank you for your word. Dear representatives of the government, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! At its 32nd meeting, the Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space considered the draft Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Protection against Ionising Radiation and Nuclear Safety, submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for consideration following a reduced procedure. In the introductory explanation, on behalf of the applicant, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Environment and Space, Robert Rožac, explained the content, objectives and reasons for the adoption of the bill. It stated that in 2018 a revised legislation on protection against ionising radiation and nuclear safety was adopted which transposed Council Directive 2013/59 Euratom on the establishment of basic safety standards for protection against ionising radiation into the Slovenian legal order. In 2019, for the first time, the law in force was amended and supplemented by the regulation of security checks on foreign nationals working in a nuclear facility in the management of radioactive substances or the transport of nuclear substances. However, due to the European Commission's formal reminder from 2020 due to the incomplete transposition of that Directive, a legislative amendment was resubmitted. The content of the draft law is therefore fully relevant to the findings of the European Commission's official reminder, due to the inconsistencies in the transposition of that Directive into the Slovenian legal order. The draft law thus includes additions to remedy infringements relating to the transposition of that Directive relating to Articles 3, 42, 49, 54 and 137 of the existing law. This is a matter of minor, materially less significant inconsistencies in the transfer. As he pointed out, the provisions on regulated professions in Articles 13, 14 and 15 of the Law had to be further amended accordingly, as they included, inter alia, references to articles which did not regulate regulated professions, and the wording of Articles 158 and 159 of the Law should be harmonised in a timely manner. He also pointed out that the provision on the verification of the effectiveness of measures to reduce radon concentrations in Article 68 of the Act and the Terminologically Harmonised Text in Articles 66, 70 and 181 of the Act was supplemented. However, a provision on inspection of construction measures for new construction and intervention in existing buildings to protect human health against the harmful effects of radon is added. The representative of the Legislative and Legal Service explained that the Legislative and Legal Service, in its written opinion on the draft law, had made minor nomotechnical comments, which had been duly taken into account by the amendments tabled by the coalition groups and by which the errors of a nomotechnical nature had also been corrected. The representative of the Commission of the Council of State for Local Self-Government and Regional Development of the State Council, Mr Bogomir Vnučec, stressed that the Commission supports the bill and has no comments on it. The Committee adopted amendments to Articles 8, 10 and 15 of the SDS, NSI and SMC. The Committee then voted on all the articles of the bill together and adopted them. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u48	Thank you for your word, chairman. Nice greetings to the secretary, secretary, colleagues and colleagues! The applicants state that the Law on Protection against Ionising Radiation and Nuclear Safety was adopted in the National Assembly in December 2017 and entered into force on 6 January 2018. The amendment of ZVISJV-1A, which entered into force on 11 May 2019, amended the law for the first time and supplemented it. The proposed amendments and additions introduce the necessary corrections, removal of false convocations, in respect of regulated professions, the wording of Articles 13, 14 and 15 of the Law should have been corrected accordingly, as they also included references to articles which do not regulate regulated professions. The basic objectives of the proposed law are to include some of the requirements of Council Directive 213/59/Euratom, which adds a new definition of the concept of radiological object, the added obligation to cooperate with authorised experts of radiation protection with authorised experts of medical physics. Nationality shall also be added to the specific data of the exposed worker; a more precise definition of the company performing the radiation activity and of the company which is the external operator of the radiation activity; a more detailed definition of the data to be reported by the external operator of the radiation activity to the operator of the installation or to the operator of the radiation activity; a more detailed definition of the content of the authorisation for the use of a highly active source of radiation. The proposed law does not prejudice the already existing basic principles of the law of the competent authorities, which are in accordance with the Resolution on Nuclear and Radiation Security in the Republic of Slovenia for the period 2013-2023. The most proposed amendments and additions to the current law are editorial in nature or are intended to remedy minor inconsistencies and shortcomings that have appeared in the application of the applicable law. Due to the proposed amendments and additions, enforcement rules will not have to be amended. The proposal was prepared in the framework of informal inter-resort cooperation and confirmed at meetings where representatives participated. We support this bill in the Slovenian Democratic Party.

The main objective of the law is to include certain requirements of the Directive. A new definition of the concept of a radiological facility is added, the mandatory cooperation of authorised experts of radiation protection with authorised experts of medical physics is added to the personal data of the exposed worker, the definition of the company carrying out the radiation activity and the information on the company which is the external operator of the radiation activity is further detailed. In particular, the determination of the data to be reported by the external operator of the radiation activity to the operator of the installation or to the operator of that radiation activity shall be specified. ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u54	Vice President, thank you again for your word. Due to a reminder from the European Commission, the Republic of Slovenia was invited to remove inconsistencies in the transposition of Council Directive 2013/59/Euratom into the Slovenian legal order. The proposal for a law therefore includes additions to remedy infringements relating to the transposition of that Directive. It is thus added to the Act that radon concentrations to check the effectiveness of measures to reduce radon concentrations should be carried out every seven years and not only once within six months of the imposition of measures or construction operations. The supervision of the operator of the compulsory public service for the management of radioactive waste shall be transferred from the energy ministry to the Ministry responsible for waste. The law shall also include the obligation to cooperate with authorised experts of radiation protection with authorised experts of medical physics where necessary. The database on the personal doses of exposed workers shall also include information on nationality and more accurate data on the company carrying out the radiation activity. It is specified that the external operator of the radiation activity must also report data on the radiation doses received in the current calendar year and in the last five calendar years in addition to the collection of personal doses of the exposed workers before the start of the work in the controlled area to the operator of the installation or to the operator of the radiation activity. The authorisation to carry out the radiation activity of a highly active source of radiation should, in a new manner, also contain minimum criteria for the efficiency of the source, the source container and additional equipment. In addition, provisions on inspection of construction measures for new construction and intervention in existing buildings to protect human health against the harmful effects of radon are added. As this is a matter of building measures, either the construction method, the preparation of the foundations and so on, it is an inspection of the work of construction inspectors. Members of New Slovenia – Christian Democrats will certainly support the bill. Our guide is that we must strive to explore and develop human and environmentally friendly technologies. In addition to solar and wind energy, the opportunities for Slovenia are seen mainly in the better use of water energy and the use of wood biomass. We are also open to new technologies based on renewable energy sources. We allow the use of nuclear energy if we cannot provide sufficient energy from more environmentally friendly sources for the development of the country. In doing so, it is essential to take into account the maximum safety criteria and throughout the process it is necessary to choose such technologies that reduce the amount of nuclear waste to a minimum. Thank you very much.

In 2019, the law was amended to regulate the security checks on foreigners as a result of the European Commission's official reminder and the law should be amended and amended from 2020. In view of the incomplete transposition of the Directive of the European Union laying down basic safety standards for the prevention of ionising radiation hazards, the proposal for a law removes reported infringements, such as terminology harmonisation, elimination of false calls on regulated professions and the like. This Directive lays down uniform basic safety standards for the protection of the health of individuals, the proportion of occupational exposure, exposure to the public and health exposure to ionising radiation risks. The proposal for a law specifies more precisely the information on the company carrying out the radiation activity and the information on the company which is the external operator of the radiation activity and the concept of a radiological facility. The proposal also adds the obligation to cooperate with authorised experts of radiation protection with authorised experts of medical physics and to add citizenship to the personal data of the exposed worker. Ionising radiation is therefore used in many fields in medicine, industry and research. Due to the nature of the regulation of the law, namely to minimise harm to human health and to radioactive contamination of the living environment, strict restrictions and precision are essential in this area. As a directive, the draft law sets out in more detail the content of the authorisation for the use of a highly active source of radiation and the data to be reported by the external operator of the radiation activity to the operator of the radiation activity. A change is also proposed, linked to the competence of the Ministry of Business Control of the statutory public economic service operator for the management of radioactive waste. ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u62	Dear Vice President! Dear Members and Members! The motion for a resolution on the general long-term development and equipment programme of the Slovenian Armed Forces by 2035 is the basic and highest development guidance document of the Slovenian Armed Forces, with which the National Assembly sets out the direction of its development and equipment in the period up to 2035. The resolution currently in force was adopted in 2010 during the period when we faced the economic and financial crisis. disproportionate interventions into the defence budget have plunged the Slovenian Army into a period of regression, as long-term and medium-term targets have not been implemented. However, the financial situation was not followed by significant structural adjustments to scale and structure. This has resulted in a number of structural and developmental deficits, reflected by the current staffing and material situation of the Slovenian Armed Forces and limited opportunities for carrying out its basic mission, the defence of the country. The motion for a resolution represents the continuity of all previous efforts to improve its defence capacity. The legal and other changes and adjustments in the defence area, such as the 2016 Strategic Defence Project, the 2019 National Security Strategy and the 2020 White Paper on Defence, are taken into account. The drafting of the resolution also included a wide range of stakeholders in the defence and security fields. When reviewing the trends and characteristics of the security environment, we cannot be overwhelmed by utopian naivety, but by sober realism. In doing so, I must point out the arc of instability both in the east and in the south of the European continent. The consequences have already been directly felt in us through increased migratory pressures. Taking into account the experience and difficulties of global change forecasting, the best response to strategic surprises is a well-prepared and responsive defence force. To this end, in preparing the motion for a resolution, we have made every effort to address the challenges facing the Slovenian Armed Forces in an integrated and systematic manner. A number of measures and solutions are identified for its conceptual, functional and organisational structural modernisation and guidance for the construction of modern military capabilities, which will enable the Slovenian Army to fulfil its statutory tasks and also to assume international commitments. Among the measures that we want to strengthen and guide the long-term development of the Slovenian Armed Forces, I must point out in particular seven systemic measures that are crucial to improving the readiness of the Slovenian Armed Forces. First, the establishment of more capable military capabilities. Adapting the scope, structure and organisation of the Slovenian Armed Forces by creating a full core of priority forces. Establishment of a spatial organisation of the Slovenian Armed Forces for the ability to increase forces. Strengthening the professionalism of military ethics of leadership. Improving the legal status of members due to the specificities of the military profession. Sixth, upgrade of the military education and training system. Seventh, last, preparation and implementation of modern strategies, doctrines, tactics and procedures. In doing so, we developed measures based on realistic assumptions and actual social capabilities and, despite the current state of the Slovenian Armed Forces, designed measures in a manner that is timely and, above all, realistic. The measures will be implemented in three stages or periods of development. First to include 2026. The second between 2027 and 2030. The third between 2031 and 2035. The development of the capacity of the Slovenian Armed Forces will focus on enhancing its fighting capacity, strength and readiness, with priority for the establishment of a medium infantry battalion equipped with 8X8 armoured vehicles by 2026. This will be the core of the Central Battalion Battle Group. After 2027, support modules of the fighting group, such as artillery, air defence, military engineering and other support elements, will be updated or built. After 2030, it will form a medium-sized reconnaissance battalion. The Slovenian army is in the service of the country and it is right to provide it with adequate conditions and resources for the sovereign defence of the state and citizens, which is also its legal task. We have in mind, in particular, sufficient, predictable and stable and long-term sustainable provision of financial resources for its provision and development. The resolution also brings a change in the scope and structure of the Slovenian Army. The total peace-keeping coverage of the Slovenian Armed Forces is planned to reach up to 10,000 members and members, with a final ratio of 7 000 permanent members and up to 3,000 reserve members, with that ratio being achieved gradually. Dear Members and Members! The motion for a resolution before you is set ambitiously while taking into account the real capacities and needs of the Republic of Slovenia. We have joined the preparations with a sincere desire and commitment to strengthening the Slovenian Army, its readiness and equipment with this security and defence of the Republic of Slovenia. We believe that the Slovenian soldier deserves quality, safety and technological advanced equipment, and that he must be supported by a professional, professional and effective military organisation in carrying out his duties. For this reason, I propose that you support the motion for a resolution. Thank you very much for your attention.

Mr Tonin, who presented the main objectives and main solutions to the motion for a resolution. The resolution on the overall long-term programme for the development and equipment of the Slovenian Armed Forces by 2035 is, as Minister Tonin said, the highest development guidance document, which plans to achieve the necessary stage in the long term for the development of the organisation and modernisation of the forces and the capacity and connectivity of the Slovenian Armed Forces to achieve national interests and defence objectives in the modern security environment. The resolution foresees the multi-phase development of the Slovenian Army to speed up the elimination of the accumulated developmental backlogs of the past decade and directs the development of the Slovenian Army to the establishment of more capable and effective military capabilities. Key comment from the written opinion of the Legal and Legislative Service presented by Mag. Sonja Bien Karlovšek refers to the content and nature of the resolution as an unlawful act of the National Assembly. The resolution is defined in the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly as an act by which the National Assembly assesses the situation, sets out the policy and adopts national programmes in specific areas. The resolution is therefore a political and, in part, an expert act, which represents general political orientations in a particular area, which is also legally regulated, has no legal effect, but its power as all political acts of the National Assembly derives from the authority of the legislator, namely the position of the National Assembly as legislator in the state regulation. The characteristics of the resolutions have not been properly taken into account in the drafting of the text of this resolution, which is attributed to the excessive degree of specificity of the motion for a resolution, which raises the issue of its compliance with the substance of the resolution as a policy-making act. Brigadier Robert Urbanč, Deputy Chief of General Staff of the Slovenian Armed Forces, said that the resolution on the general long-term programme for the development and equipping of the Slovenian Armed Forces is a key document with which the National Assembly defines the objectives, path and resources for the further development of the Slovenian Armed Forces. He stressed, however, that the proposed resolution was thoughtful and necessary and that it was a military profession. The Council of State and the President of the Commission of the National Council of State Rajko Fajt said that the Commission for State Arrangements at its meeting had considered and supported the motion for a resolution. In the debate, the panellists from the coalition parliamentary groups supported the motion for a resolution and the opposition Members objected to the motion for a resolution or were detained until the motion for a resolution. Coalition MPs were of the opinion that a regulated state must have the military and the resources to ensure that the military is preserved, which the current government is working towards. The opposition Members questioned the implementation of the resolution and wondered where the country would receive funds for its implementation. Following the discussion, the Committee adopted all amendments to the SDS, NSI and SMC groups. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on all chapters of the motion for a resolution and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the supplementary motion for a resolution, which includes the amendments adopted, is prepared on the basis of Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The updated motion for a resolution is an integral part of this report. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u86	Mr President, honourable Members and honourable Members! The right to personal assistance is a major civilisation achievement in Slovenia and also wider. Living alone and independently was the purpose of the Personal Assistance Act, which significantly improved the lives of users, those with disabilities who need very much support for their own lives. While the implementation of personal assistance in Slovenia is not new, it did not start with the adoption of the law, but before that personal assistance was funded and implemented programmely. However, due to limited resources as a programme, not all users could receive the sufficient number of hours of assistance they needed. As a rule, they got it much less. It was also not possible for all users in need of personal assistance to be provided. With the adoption of the Personal Assistance Act 2017, personal assistance has become a right. All users are entitled to it under the same conditions regardless of their property situation and, of course, personal assistance is not a social adjustment or transfer. That is why the Law on Personal Assistance is a major step forward in the development of disability protection. The law has placed the user at the centre, the scope of the service is determined according to its needs, the user chooses the operator of the personal assistance, the user must have control over the organisation and design or implementation of the assistance according to his/her own needs, abilities, living circumstances, lifestyle, conditions and wishes. Thus, the law in some special way enforces the fundamental human rights of persons with disabilities and, of course, also the objectives of the United Nations Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities. We are therefore aware at the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities or at the Government that the Law on Personal Assistance or Personal Assistance is vital to ensuring the independent life of persons with disabilities and their equal inclusion in society. However, we are also aware that in the implementation of the law in practice it has been shown that some provisions are too general, that individual solutions are inconsistently defined and therefore there is a lack of clarity and also a variety of means of implementation. For example, a personal assistance provider may be an independent entrepreneur, which is contrary to the definition of personal assistance as non-profit activities. Nor does the law provide for sufficient detailed conditions for obtaining the status of the operator. Personal assistance is further defined by the definition of assistance for self-employment and active participation in society, but a greater number of family members may also be employed for the purpose of solving social problems as personal assistants for all hours authorised. So this is about solving family economic and social problems. The training of all stakeholders in personal assistance is also inadequate; finally, the list of members of the professional committees for the assessment of the right to personal assistance is too extensive and the final opinions on the same needs of beneficiaries may vary in different parts of Slovenia. For all of this, we have joined the Ministry or Government in amending the Personal Assistance Act before you. We listened in particular to the warnings of users of personal assistance. For us it is crucial what the user needs and what they themselves have estimated that it is not in the most appropriate way regulated by the applicable law. All our efforts lead to improving the current legal solutions to ensure the best possible implementation of personal assistance in favour of users to ensure independent independent living. Therefore, the support of you, Members of Parliament and Members of the National Assembly in the adoption of this law is still important. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u92	Thank you, President, for your word. Dear colleagues, distinguished colleagues, distinguished Secretary of State! Social Democrats will not oppose the proposed amendments to the Personal Assistance Act. We estimate that this is an attempt to correct the shortcomings of the personal assistance system. Unfortunately, the proposal is not systematic and comprehensive. There are still a number of open questions that are not resolved in this law. At the time of the adoption of the proposal for a law in 2017, which introduced personal assistance for us, we warned the Social Democrats that it would be better if we regulated a uniform and comprehensive system of assistance for persons who are long-term and permanently insolvent in carrying out basic daily activities and consequently in social inclusion. Such a system would represent an integrated provision of personal assistance services and long-term persons. Thus, due to the adoption of specific laws, we now have a number of different arrangements regulating similar living conditions differently; especially with regard to the status of persons with disabilities who provide care and assistance. Parents who care for their child with special needs receive only partial payment of lost income under the Parental Protection Act and family benefits at minimum wage. Family helpers who care for a stable person in their home environment also receive only partial compensation for the loss of income of EUR 751. On the other hand, personal assistants shall have all rights arising from a working relationship. It belongs to the 23rd-paid class, vacation, regress, lunch payment, transportation and hospital. It is precisely because of the better status of personal assistants that it has proved in practice that many family assistants prefer to arrange for the status of a personal assistant, even though individual recipients of personal assistance services do not need a personal assistant, or at least not to the extent they receive it, but only care and protection. The result of this situation was an exceptional increase in the number of eligible persons with disabilities who received the right to personal assistance and a very rapid increase in budgetary expenditure from the original 8 million in 2017 to the current 83 million euro in 2021. The exceptional growth in budgetary expenditure today is essentially the reason why we are adopting amendments to the Personal Assistance Act, as some money is expected to be saved, so a reassessment of the needs of persons with disabilities is envisaged in order to reduce the number of hours of offering personal assistance. Some disabled persons will lose their personal assistant status and will have to re-establish their family assistant status, and the number of family members who may be the personal assistants of their relatives will be limited to two members. Personal assistants who so far have offered services alone through espionage will have to join a major personal assistant after a new one. Social Democrats will continue to support the functioning of personal assistance, as it contributes significantly to the independent lives of people with disabilities. A personal assistant allows them to live in a community and to decide their own lives in the same way as others. Since personal assistance is an independent life of persons with disabilities, services are provided wherever users need them for their activities and inclusion in society. Personal assistance services thus enable users to realize their personal desires and interests either in a professional or in a leisurely area. Despite all the positive consequences that personal assistance has for users, it will continue to be developed and upgraded, particularly in relation to the long-term system, in such a way that it will be financially sustainable and will not disrupt the relationship between personal assistants and family assistants. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u94	Thank you so much for saying that. Dear Members and Members of the Ministry, good afternoon! The Personal Assistance Act was adopted in February 2017. We did not oppose him in Levica because we advocate the right of a comprehensive personal assistant as a universal right for all, regardless of property or income. The good part of this law is that it defined the guaranteed right to personal assistance, which was a step forward from the point of zero. It was also positive that deaf people without the right to an allowance for foreign aid and service received at least a communication allowance. At that time, however, we had pointed out a major error in the drafting of this law, which has also proved to be crucial in practice. Because the law fully liberalized the field of personal assistance, and the profession of a personal assistant was prefigured. Virtually everyone could become both an operator and an assistant without adequate training or supervision. The current proposal seeks to partially remedy some mistakes, but it also creates new ones. First of all, in Levica, we are appalled at how badly the marriage is prepared. According to the SPS, which has serious comments on virtually all articles, this amendment is constitutionally controversial, vague, non-systematic, opaque, unfounded, incompatible with other laws, linguisticly inadequate and inconsistent with the applicable Personal Assistance Act. Moreover, due to the limitation of one third of the articles recorded, the law was broken and made completely imprecise. The coalition then voted on a reduced procedure, although the conditions were not met at all, since there were no minor changes. That's why we can't support him in Levica. In preparing this law, the government has dealt irresponsibly with users — people with disabilities who need personal assistance as well as with workers — personal assistants who provide assistance to those in need every day. We also cannot support the law in Levica, which foresees that personal assistance will be reduced by €10 million each year in the coming years. In this regard, we also propose an amendment that personal assistants should be treated normally as all other workers and that they should be regularly employed and recognised as workers' rights. There is no reason for personal assistants to be understood as entrepreneurs, but only and only as workers who belong to them, such as protection against dismissal, leave, minimum wages, regress; in short, all rights under labour law, which otherwise belong to workers. The amendment also proposes that the user's personal assistance may be performed by a maximum of one family member, as proposed by the expert group and as specified in the first version of the novel. In order to determine that personal assistants can also be family members, two problems have arisen. There has been a conflict of interest between providing income to the family and the needs of the user and a multi-layered problem in the ability of family members to provide personal and emotional autonomy. Family members have a close personal relationship with the person on whom their income depends. Families are thus crucified during the use of the Institute of Personal Assistant to resolve their own family financial distress and, on the other hand, during the use of the Institute for the actual benefit of the user. We have insisted in Levica in 2017 and today we insist that personal assistance should be understood as a de facto public service and that it is necessary to prevent the establishment of “enterprises” that would carry out personal assistance. Our calls then and this time came across a deaf ear, so we will not support the law. Thank you very much.

In the case of personal assistants, personal assistants will be able to carry out their work on the basis of a contract of employment or as self-employed individuals, but not more than 40 hours per week. A maximum of 2 family members of the user will be able to perform the work of a personal assistant. The news is that the personal assistance provider must have at least 10 users. All contractors will have to have an employed professional leader and larger contractors will also have to have a coordinator of personal assistance. However, in order to avoid the abuse that is taking place, to ensure that care is provided twice at the same time, personal assistance will be excluded through institutional protection, family assistant, long-term hospital treatment, institutional protection under the rules governing school legislation, social inclusion by supporting and putting an end to prison sentences. However, it will not be excluded by the home aid service and the right to manage, protect and work under special conditions, but the approved hours of personal assistance will be reduced by the number of hours of other services granted. The same applies to hours when the user of personal assistance is involved in the implementation of the educational programme so that the hours do not double. Parliamentary Group Nova Slovenija – Christian Democrats will be unanimously supported by amendments to the Personal Assistance Act. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u98	Thank you for your word, lovely greeting to all present! The implementation of personal assistance has been under the care of both users and politicians for several years now, and we both agree that certain changes are still necessary, since, as in a number of other laws, shortcomings in implementation in practice have been demonstrated in time when the law has been able to live and show some bad pay or anomaly. First of all, it must be pointed out, which is what the bill's own proponent stresses that the exercise of personal assistance cannot in any way be profit-making. This principle has created quite a few solutions in the novel under consideration. Personal assistance will continue to be provided by humanitarian and disability organisations with acquired status, and institutions and societies will have to acquire a status of action in the public interest in the field of disability or social protection. In order to ensure quality in the exercise of personal assistance and to exclude personal assistance by earning profits, the work burdens of personal assistants who carry out their work as independent entrepreneurs shall be limited to hours as applicable for employees under the law governing working relationships. Under the agreement or under the temporary or occasional work of students and students, personal assistants will only be able to replace regular staff assistants for up to 720 hours a year. The same applies to pensioners who will only be able to replace a personal assistant in the framework of temporary and temporary work in accordance with the Labour Market Regulation Act. There is also a need for new experience or specific knowledge in the field of the exercise of personal assistance from providers of personal assistance. They will have to implement the service for at least 10 users and will also have to hire a professional leader. As experience has shown that the expert committee did not have the same evaluation criteria, the amendment provides for a reassessment of users already entitled to personal assistance. The proposal also provides for a new expert body. This will be established within the framework of the Social Protection Institute. In the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we also welcome the solution concerning the number of family members who are able to exercise personal assistance, since in accordance with the new proposal, the personal assistants will be able to no more than two family members. Some have expressed concerns about this solution because they feel that family members are less suitable for personal assistants because they do not support the user’s independence. This is where we need to keep in mind what a personal assistant is. Pursuant to the current definition, personal assistance is defined as assistance to the user in all those tasks and activities that the user cannot carry out on his own because of the type and degree of disability, but he needs them at home and outside every day in order to be independent, active and equally involved in society. We believe that sometimes the concern of family members for the health and well-being of the user can collide with the definition of personal assistance, but it is crucial that the user remains satisfied with the choice of an assistant and that he is guaranteed the maximum degree of autonomy to achieve the status of an equal member of our society. Personal assistants are not all the same as family members, but they must all seek a common denominator, which is reflected in the definition of personal assistance and the related provision of equal opportunities for users, and the achievement of legal standards. The new Law on Personal Assistance foresees some other news, we have only highlighted the key. We consider everything to be good and appropriate, but with some additions we could improve them, but a few more talks below in the debate on the amendments tabled. Today, we will support the proposed amendments and additions to the Personal Assistance Act of the Modern Centre Party Group.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u102	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Secretary of State with a co-worker, colleagues and colleagues, everyone, have a good day! In 2017, as legislators and as a society, we made an important move in the implementation of the right to personal assistance as a means of achieving an independent and quality life for individuals with long-term physical, mental, intellectual or sensory disabilities. Although the law has been in force since 2019, it has been sufficient for three years to establish the first deficiencies and anomalies of the arrangements in force requiring appropriate additions and corrections. They are, in fact, pointed out by all key stakeholders involved in the exercise of the right to personal assistance. Although changes in marriage should have taken place sooner, it is better late than never. The key red thread of this time change and complementing is the elimination of the current law's anomalies, the provision of greater quality and more than the necessary control over the exercise of personal assistance, and at the same time the greater independence and autonomy of users of personal assistance. We agree with the proposed solutions, because since 2019, when the law began to apply, his spirit has been lost and a number of abuses have been taking place. The fact is that the exercise of personal assistance has in a sense become a family business, rather than leading to the possibility of user autonomy. It is therefore right and only reasonable that personal assistance is carried out as a non-profit activity and that self-declared entrepreneurs withdraw as its potential contractors. Restrictions are also imposed under clearer conditions for training operators and for the employment of the professional manager. So far, the professional commissions have not assessed the need for personal assistance according to uniform criteria and a reassessment is also envisaged for users who are already entitled to the right. Following the new entry, only those that will be implemented for at least ten users will be allowed to enter the assistance providers' register. In our opinion, however, one of the most important novelties of the proposed law is the rule that the number of family members who may be personal assistants should be limited to two. The idea of independent life underlines that disabled people are able to manage their lives, to have the right to choose support and the right to live in a community. If someone chooses a life in a department because they don't know another one or not available, that's not a choice. The same is true if he lives at home and is cared for by his family. Therefore, a personal assistant is an institution for ensuring the independence of its users and cannot be primarily designed to address the social situation of the family. The possibility of a relative becoming a personal assistant does not extend the premises of freedom to the user, but return to the framework of the family institution. Of course, the help and support of the family is important, but it is not the material gain of other family members, but most of all, it is the loss of those who urgently need personal assistance — users. It is therefore extremely important that the law will further promote the philosophy of independent life in the future, without becoming another of those legislative frameworks that reproduce the patronage system of social protection. Since the proposed law strengthens transparency and supervision of implementation, improves the quality of personal assistance and enhances the independence of users, we will support the unconnected Members of the bill.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u130	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! You have before you the proposal for a law on debarcation, which represents the first legislative package on the way to reducing the administrative and regulatory burden in public administration and improving the efficiency of its business. Government efforts to improve the regulatory environment and consequently improve Slovenia's global competitiveness are not new. In this direction, governments have been working for almost a decade, but this time debirocraticism is also enshrined in the Coalition Treaty as a basic guide. For the first time, it is also being sought by means of a package way of dealing with simple legislative proposals that do not require systematic changes. For this purpose, last spring the Strategic Council for Debarcation, led by the President of Mag, was established. Ivan Simich. The Strategic Council has set up three working groups in the field of taxes, economics and the environment. They shall be attended by representatives of economic associations, associations of local communities, academic spheres, IT and other experts. We've received a lot of suggestions. Some are already included in this package, but they are still waiting for the next packages. The aim of the bill is thus to facilitate accessibility to public services and to eliminate the many unnecessary administrative burdens imposed by the State on citizens and citizens, economic operators, experts, sports coaches, farmers, cultural workers, officials and municipalities in their operations with the State. To this end, in the first part of the proposal, the law amends and complements 14 different laws covering the areas of the register of the population, general administrative and tax procedure, sport, agrarian communities, culture, judicial experts, interpreters and valuers, integrity, public servants and nature conservation. Another valid reason for the Government to adopt and propose to the National Assembly a proposal for a law is the international indicators. These show that Slovenia does not exploit all its potential according to the Global Competitiveness Index. In some areas, it is even highly sub-average. In 2019, the regulatory burden index took only 126th place from 141 countries. The main starting point of the second part of the bill is therefore to strengthen the rule of law by reducing the regulatory burden. To this end, more than 200 laws are repealed and mechanisms are in place to prevent further accumulation of national rules. We are aware that debirocratisation is not a process that can be completed overnight and with only one roof law. However, the first urgent stage is to identify the clear time limits and the exact extent of the irregular legal order that contained more than 30,000 legal acts before the establishment of the Strategic Council, with the oldest of 1946. The proposed law provides a legal basis for the establishment of an official register which will allow easy access and a clear review of the official texts of the State rules binding on the individual at the chosen time. I should like to conclude by thanking you for your assistance in drafting the draft law on debarcation to the President of the Strategic Debarcation Council, which unfortunately does not allow the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly to participate in the general debate; however, he will participate in the discussion at the meeting of the home working body. I would also like to thank the members of the Strategic Council, the working groups and all the ministries and government services that have contributed their proposals and made it possible for us to have a bill on the table today. I also thank the economic associations that have sent a letter of support and the associations of local communities offering a deadline for further cooperation. Dear Members, we have adopted the proposed law in the Government of the Republic of Slovenia because we believe that Slovenia urgently needs it for the development breakthrough, so please support it. Thank you very much.

The administrative environment is among the key obstacles to the accelerated development of Slovenia in all areas. Complicated and delayed procedures hoard better the benefits of all our potentials, while needlessly burdening the public officials who run these procedures. In the first mandate, the modern centre's client has already worked as a priority to remove administrative barriers and de-de-barcation, as well as to make public authorities more accessible and responsive. Some see problems in a large number of civil servants, but the real problem is ineffective procedures, which, with many steps, practically steal time from both representatives of the public sector and especially from citizens and representatives of the economy. For this reason, we will only reach an administrativeally more prosperous country by rounding up the digitisation of the country, speeding up procedures, eliminating unnecessary duplication of activities and allowing public officials to devote their time to the content of the operation rather than the bureaucratic procedure. Let us mention some of the more important solutions to the proposal for a law on debarcation that is ahead of us. In order to enable the speedier and more efficient operation of officials with individuals, data on mobile numbers and e-mail address are added to the Residence Registration Act and the Central Population Register Act, of course, provided that both data are transmitted voluntarily. In the General Administrative Procedure Act, it is also possible to send notices of a consignment via a text message and the secretaries of the State will be able to sign the decisions in individual procedures instead of the Minister. The Public Servants Act also allows public servants to be served on official e-mail. Finally! These are just a few solutions for simplifying the life and business of citizens and thus contributing to lowering costs and administrative barriers. Of course, with full attention that the services of the state administration are safe, fast and to the satisfaction of citizens and customers in administrative procedures. Slovenia has already taken major steps in the area of integration of records and databases, which has saved users of public services time spent on various administrative procedures. In the Modern Centre Party, we are working towards the digital integration of all national authorities and the rationalisation of all regulations. We are convinced that the basic problem, as mentioned above, is the duplication of staff and that greater transparency of the functioning of the public authorities itself needs to be ensured. We put first the measures and projects that will make it easier for citizens to obtain documents, consents and permits in all the procedures in which they deal with the country. Projects are under way to establish a modern e-Identity that will enable citizens to have an easy digital relationship with national authorities. It will also be an important step for businesses to implement an e-Identity project with all the data in one place, allowing companies to submit reports more easily and to run for or for development funds. These projects will allow citizens, businesses, public servants to deal solely with content and development, but not with so-called late administrative procedures. In the future, special attention will also be paid to the area of accommodation in the space and construction of buildings, where much has already been done with the eSpace project. The continuation of digitisation with the EMOP project will first allow for the full protection of the natural environment, as it will automatically prevent interventions that could actually threaten it. In addition, the digitisation of the area of spatial governance will achieve full transparency and great acceleration of all procedures, as it will eliminate the late repetition of one and the same steps in terms of conditions, consensus and various permits. Digitalisation of space management is one of our key programme priorities, as it is seen as one of the key problems by citizens, especially the economy and various investors. We support today's proposal for a law on debarcation, which is at first reading, in the Joint Meeting of the Modern Centre, and we will continue to seek solutions in the area of the elimination of administrative barriers, so that only life in the framework of civil activities and rights and business operations will be simplified. Thank you for your attention.

It includes both acts of the Republic of Slovenia, including jurisprudence, as well as acts of the acquis of the European Union. ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u148	Nicely greeted. Dear Members, Members, President, Secretary of State! That Slovenia has been very liberal, full of administrative obstacles, it has been known for a long time, despite the fact that since 2004 we have been working through the Sector for the Elimination of Administrative Obstacles of the Ministry of Public Administration in Slovenia in this area. In the course of the day-to-day meeting with citizens, entrepreneurs, representatives of economic associations, farmers are all united in the area of administrative barriers: the barrier is too much, the procedures for obtaining permits and the agreement is under way. Also on a number of competitiveness scales, economic freedom scales Slovenia has poor cities due mainly to excessive regulation, too many regulations and too long procedures. For all the reasons mentioned in the SDS, we have already convened an extraordinary meeting of the National Assembly in the previous mandate in 2017 and have put forward a number of concrete proposals for de-liberation of the country. Something has not changed much since then, so, of course, in the SDS Group, we welcome the proposal for a law on debarcation, which is before us today. The basic aim of the law is to simplify, simplify the lives of citizens and make it easier for businesses to operate and thus contribute to reducing costs and removing administrative barriers. Only less burdensome legislation will improve the competitiveness of the economy and, of course, consequently, improve the standard of citizens. The law before us is made up of two parts. The first part contains amendments and additions to laws that do not require systematic amendments to legislation, while the second part includes the termination of laws and their non-applicable acts. In Slovenia we have exceptional regulatory density, we have about 6,000 700 laws, of which more than 40 percent are invalid. We have about 45,000 sub-laws, about 38 percent of which are invalid. All of this is extremely imprudent for citizens. On the basis of the existing register of rules, citizens and other entities find it very difficult to determine which rules apply to them. It is also common for the entry into force and entry into force of a law or a sub-law to be different, and it is quite unusual for them to remain in force under the law, even though their legal basis is repealed. Contentual simplifications relate to fourteen laws. I will continue to highlight some of the major changes. In order to enable the speedier and more efficient operation of officials with individuals, data on mobile numbers and e-mail addresses are added in the Residence Registration Act and the Central Population Register Act. Both data will be transmitted voluntarily by the citizen. The service of documents or acts issued by the State Administration shall be simplified, instead of being made by regular mail, by means of an e-mail address which will be linked to a telephone number, provided that the individual decides to do so. It'll be a voluntary choice. However, it means a substantial simplification, since you will no longer have to go to the post office and take over the shipments there. Decisions of public servants may be served on official e-mail. The stamp and the writing of the date shall be removed with the word on the hotline, and the notification of the consignment sent shall also be made available via an SMS message. The decisions will also be signed by the State secretaries following the new ones, which will certainly burden the ministers with their work. In tax procedures, it is possible that the taxable person is an agrarian community, not all of its members. It will be possible for the founder to carry out the tasks of carrying out the insurance area on his own, meaning that this will allow for the exercise of the environmental control of the municipal and inter-agency administrations as well. With regard to the Integrity and Prevention of Corruption Act, the Government proposes that the tenderer's statements of non-relationship with the chairman of a public person should no longer be obtained in the process of concluding each contract, but only in the case of a concession of a public-private partnership and public procurement. In the Sports Act, double verification of the fulfilment of the conditions of age and at least secondary vocational education in the entry into the register of professional workers in sport is hereby suspended. The law also introduces the establishment of an official register of regulations that will make legislation more transparent as all official texts of national rules will be published in one place. The Official Register will contain all the applicable national rules, including the laws we adopt in the National Assembly, and the regulations adopted by the Government. It will also be done to purify laws and acts that are no longer in force. For today there are still and even there are under-laws that are based on laws that are no longer in force. Funding for the gradual establishment of the official register will be provided over the next five years from EU funds, specifically from the Recovery and Resistance Fund. In the second part of the law, more than 200 laws and related acts are repealed, which are no longer applicable. In so doing, the application of Article 154 of the Constitution, which requires publication in the Official Journal also before the expiry of the national rules, shall be subject to a mechanism for the cleansing of the acquis which will prevent the accumulation of rules which are no longer applicable due to the time elapsed or other changes. Of course, many changes are still needed. This is the first such law, and I believe that this is just the beginning and, of course, the first time to de-de-irocraticize the country. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we will, of course, support this law at first reading. Thank you.

This is not true, since the Official Journal remains unchanged and is intended for the first time to inform individuals of a regulation before it enters into force and the rules are drawn up in the chronological order and the rules will be collected in the official register by hierarchy and subject matter and will allow permanent access by individuals to the applicable legal order. Nor has it been repeatedly made clear that the management of the official register will be directed to an economic company. That's not true. The law clearly states that the official registry administrator will be the Official Gazette itself. And in fact, the official register will only mean the fourth collection in the management of the Official Journal. They are already managing three collections, this is the Official Gazette — the regulatory part, the Official Gazette — the announcing part and the Official Gazette — the international treaties. Officially refined texts will be published in this fourth part, since, as you know, most of the time, only marriage novels are being adopted and much less whole laws are being adopted. However, as regards the added value of the existing legal and information system, the main difference is that the existing legal and information system is of an information nature, where it clearly states that the State does not assume any responsibility, not for compensation or any other liability for the correctness of the content in this information system. However, the new official register will be subject to the principle of trust, so citizens will not have to bear the harmful consequences if it turns out that there has been any error in linking the matter with the basic law.
