In the existing regulation, it is possible for the State secretaries to sign the decision, but only those with the appropriate education and professional examination. Now this option is equivalent to that of all state secretaries who are officials. The person who will run the procedure must in any event have a professional examination. Then it's about the sense of breaking the law. Here I would correct the statement that illegal laws are being repealed. That's not true. Laws still in force are being repealed, only applied no more. The Constitution requires that laws be repealed by that State authority, which has also adopted them. It's the National Choir. The repeal must also be published in the Official Journal, allowing individuals to have a clear view on the exact date of expiry of the law. This also gives the possibility to refer to that Official Gazette where this law is mentioned. So far, however, there has been practice that laws have been reviewed in the executive branch, where they have only indicated that laws no longer apply. However, individuals have not been aware of who has made such a decision, since when such a law is no longer applicable, or whether such a decision has legal consequences. The essential difference is that now all laws are repealed in the National Assembly, that each branch of government assumes responsibility for its area of competence. You had some other questions. I believe that the National Assembly is the first and main area to be called for a debate on various current social issues, but today we are addressing the reasons, objectives and fundamental solutions of the draft law on debarcation, so I should be allowed not to respond to questions that are not related to this topic or to questions that are too general to be answered at all without further explanation. ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u237	The Pharmacy Act, as it exists at this time, is something we support in Levica. It introduces two and only two. Firstly, two pharmacy velodrogerists, who are in municipal ownership, are allowed to stay in municipal property and, despite the ban on vertical connections between suppliers and pharmacies, will not be sold off to private individuals. However, another provision that we have entered into the law says that all possible surpluses arising from pharmacy activities must also be returned to the basic activity. We take medicines and their supplies as human rights, as something that is done for the needs of the community, to help people who are not healthy, and not as a normal business in which profits are generated on the burdens and needs of people. The original law wasn't like that. The original law provided for deregulation of so-called gravitational zones, so-called regulation of pharmacies, how many can we have in a particular area, who can set up, open, manage and so on. Fortunately, in the previous procedure, we pushed these provisions out of the law – “Yes!”. However, for the last time, with the amendment of the SDS and New Slovenia connection, it is practically the same to return to the law. The consequences of deregulation of pharmacies and their cities in the community can be seen by many abroad. In cities, for the first time, we can expect an increase in competition, a rise in pharmacies and a consequent pressure on all pharmacies to sell more medicines just so that profits can be reversed. In the countryside, on the other hand, we can expect the closure of pharmacies because, under the pressure of competition, some pharmacies, which now exist in public or concessional form, will be without such an important financial flow for the bare operation. The only winners of this type of structure will be large individuals who will be able to monopolize drugstore activities in our country under such deregulation. And, of course, we will vote against this decision in Levica. If you, ladies and gentlemen, are concerned that some part of Slovenia is too remote from the central pharmacy even in the existing law, there are commissions allowing you to open a pharmacy branch. There is no need for deregulation if we are only honest about the supply of medicines. But if we believe in the supply of medicines as a business to be earned, then we get amendments like this to Article 4. We will vote against this amendment in the left hand side.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-10-20-SDZ8-Redna-26.ana.u243	Thank you very much again, Vice President. With the amendment tabled by the applicants to Article 6 of the proposal for a pharmacy act, they want the applicant to terminate the operation of two of his own suppliers of medicines and painkillers, which have a total market share of 30% in the Republic of Slovenia. These are the Maribor Farmadent and the Ljubljana-based LL Grossist. Their presence ensures at least minimal competition in this market and thus lower prices. What is essential is that their profits remain fully in public pharmacy institutions for their developmental needs and for the education of pharmacists. It is unreasonable and irresponsible to propose an amendment which, on the one hand, removes well-functioning own suppliers from which operations are of exclusive benefit to the local community and the public pharmacy system, as the resulting profit is returned to meeting the needs of citizens and employees. On the other hand, this amendment removes the minimum competition between suppliers and leaves the supply of medicinal products entirely to foreigners and legal persons with hidden owners. Explanation that maintaining the activities of Pharmadent and Grossista constitutes unfair competition – how can anyone even write this down if the Pharmadent has been operating since 1972 in Slovenia and Grossist since 2009? Where has all these years been unfair competition, and today this is one of the key reasons on the part of the proposer? The reference to the 2019 Constitutional Decision is simply misleading the applicant. The Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia has found that the vertical link is not inconsistent with the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. The legislator can change the existing regime in a way that is in line with European legislation and which enables the promotion of public health to be achieved, and pharmacies are part of public health, so that the public health sector can also generate income in this area. We therefore proposed Article 6 in the SMC, which fully follows Directive No 24 and regulates the so-called in-house public procurement. It is clearly the applicant's interest to phase out the well-functioning public pharmacy system and to place foreign pharmacy owners in its place by privatising to them all the profits. We oppose this amendment.

The fact is that on the Slovenian drug supply market we have almost four suppliers of Chemofarmation, Salus, Pharmadent and Grossist. With the continued operation of both own suppliers, at least minimal competition in the supply market in the Republic of Slovenia is ensured, resulting in lower prices for medicines and other products in pharmacies. In 2019 the Competition Protection Agency as well as the Institute for Health Insurance of the Republic of Slovenia drew attention to this fact. Both pointed out that there was a need for greater competition between drug suppliers in order to ensure lower prices and thus greater savings in public funds for medicinal products. Therefore, Your Honor, in the SMC Group we support the bill. Thank you very much.
